Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Polycarp loves geometric progressions very much. Since he was only three years old, he loves only the progressions of length three. He also has a favorite integer k and a sequence a, consisting of n integers.

He wants to know how many subsequences of length three can be selected from a, so that they form a geometric progression with common ratio k.

A subsequence of length three is a combination of three such indexes i1, i2, i3, that 1 ≤ i1 < i2 < i3 ≤ n. That is, a subsequence of length three are such groups of three elements that are not necessarily consecutive in the sequence, but their indexes are strictly increasing.

A geometric progression with common ratio k is a sequence of numbers of the form b·k0, b·k1, ..., b·kr - 1.

Polycarp is only three years old, so he can not calculate this number himself. Help him to do it.

Input

The first line of the input contains two integers, n and k (1 ≤ n, k ≤ 2·105), showing how many numbers Polycarp's sequence has and his favorite number.

The second line contains n integers a1, a2, ..., an ( - 109 ≤ ai ≤ 109) — elements of the sequence.

Output

Output a single number — the number of ways to choose a subsequence of length three, such that it forms a geometric progression with a common ratio k.

Examples

Input

5 2
1 1 2 2 4


Output

4

Input

3 1
1 1 1


Output

1

Input

10 3
1 2 6 2 3 6 9 18 3 9


Output

6

Note

In the first sample test the answer is four, as any of the two 1s can be chosen as the first element, the second element can be any of the 2s, and the third element of the subsequence must be equal to 4.

The input will be given via stdin and the output should be printed to stdout by your code.

Now solve the problem by providing the code.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

We need to find the number of subsequences of length 3 that form a geometric progression with common ratio k.

A geometric progression with ratio k has the form: b, b*k, b*k²

So for three elements at positions i < j < k, we need:
- a[i] = b
- a[j] = b * k  
- a[k] = b * k² = (b * k) * k = a[j] * k

## Approach

For each middle element at position j, I need to count:
- How many elements before j have value equal to a[j] / k
- How many elements after j have value equal to a[j] * k

The total count for this middle position j would be: (count of elements before j with value a[j]/k) × (count of elements after j with value a[j]*k)

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Handle special cases**:
   - If k = 1, then we're looking for three equal elements (since b, b, b)
   - If k = 0, then we need to be careful about division by zero

2. **For general k**:
   - For each middle element at position j, calculate how many valid first and third elements exist
   - First element should have value = a[j] / k
   - Third element should have value = a[j] * k

3. **Implementation strategy**:
   - Use a frequency map to count occurrences of each value
   - For each position, calculate the required values and count valid combinations

## Edge Cases

- k = 1: All elements are equal, we need combinations of three equal elements
- k = 0: Only valid progressions are (b, 0, 0) for b ≠ 0
- Division by k: Only valid if a[j] is divisible by k

```python
n, k = map(int, input().split())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

if k == 1:
    # For k = 1, we need three equal elements
    from collections import Counter
    count = Counter(a)
    result = 0
    for val, freq in count.items():
        if freq >= 3:
            result += freq * (freq - 1) * (freq - 2) // 6
    print(result)
else:
    # For k != 1
    result = 0
    
    # Count frequencies of all elements
    from collections import Counter
    freq = Counter(a)
    
    # For each possible middle element value
    for j in range(n):
        mid_val = a[j]
        
        # First element should be mid_val / k
        first_val = mid_val // k
        
        # Third element should be mid_val * k
        third_val = mid_val * k
        
        # Check if division is valid (mid_val must be divisible by k)
        if mid_val % k != 0:
            continue
            
        # Count how many elements before j have value first_val
        before_count = 0
        for i in range(j):
            if a[i] == first_val:
                before_count += 1
        
        # Count how many elements after j have value third_val
        after_count = 0
        for i in range(j + 1, n):
            if a[i] == third_val:
                after_count += 1
        
        result += before_count * after_count
    
    print(result)
```

Wait, this solution might be too slow for large inputs (O(n²)). Let me optimize it.

```python
n, k = map(int, input().split())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

if k == 1:
    # For k = 1, we need three equal elements
    from collections import Counter
    count = Counter(a)
    result = 0
    for val, freq in count.items():
        if freq >= 3:
            result += freq * (freq - 1) * (freq - 2) // 6
    print(result)
else:
    # For k != 1
    result = 0
    
    # We'll process each position as the middle element
    # For each middle position j, we need:
    # - Before j: elements with value a[j]/k
    # - After j: elements with value a[j]*k
    
    # Precompute prefix counts (how many times each value appears before position j)
    prefix_count = {}
    # Precompute suffix counts (how many times each value appears after position j)
    suffix_count = {}
    
    # Initialize suffix count with all elements
    for val in a:
        suffix_count[val] = suffix_count.get(val, 0) + 1
    
    # Process each position as middle
    for j in range(n):
        mid_val = a[j]
        
        # Check if division by k is valid
        if mid_val % k != 0:
            # No valid progression with this middle element
            pass
        else:
            first_val = mid_val // k
            third_val = mid_val * k
            
            # Update suffix count (remove current element)
            suffix_count[mid_val] = suffix_count.get(mid_val, 0) - 1
            if suffix_count[mid_val] == 0:
                del suffix_count[mid_val]
            
            # Count valid combinations
            before_count = prefix_count.get(first_val, 0)
            after_count = suffix_count.get(third_val, 0)
            
            result += before_count * after_count
        
        # Add current element to prefix count
        prefix_count[mid_val] = prefix_count.get(mid_val, 0) + 1
    
    print(result)
```

a = list(map(int, input().split()))

if k == 1:
    # For k = 1, we need three equal elements
    from collections import Counter
    count = Counter(a)
    result = 0
    for val, freq in count.items():
        if freq >= 3:
            result += freq * (freq - 1) * (freq - 2) // 6
    print(result)
else:
    # For k != 1
    result = 0
    
    # We'll process each position as the middle element
    # For each middle position j, we need:
    # - Before j: elements with value a[j]/k
    # - After j: elements with value a[j]*k
    
    # Precompute prefix counts (how many times each value appears before position j)
    prefix_count = {}
    # Precompute suffix counts (how many times each value appears after position j)
    suffix_count = {}
    
    # Initialize suffix count with all elements
    for val in a:
        suffix_count[val] = suffix_count.get(val, 0) + 1
    
    # Process each position as middle
    for j in range(n):
        mid_val = a[j]
        
        # Check if division by k is valid
        if mid_val % k != 0:
            # No valid progression with this middle element
            pass
        else:
            first_val = mid_val // k
            third_val = mid_val * k

            suffix_count[mid_val] = suffix_count.get(mid_val, 0) - 1
            if suffix_count[mid_val] == 0:
                del suffix_count[mid_val]
            
            # Count valid combinations
            before_count = prefix_count.get(first_val, 0)
            after_count = suffix_count.get(third_val, 0)
            
            result += before_count * after_count
        
        # Add current element to prefix count
        prefix_count[mid_val] = prefix_count.get(mid_val, 0) + 1
    
    print(result)
